Remark C.4

(using the A-model localization)
.

Note that the stable envelope we have constructed is written in the stable basis, which is a basis for the equivariant quantum cohomology of the Higgs branch of the gauge theory. The quantum equivariant cohomology consists of the vector space of the underlying classical equivariant cohomology together with a deformed ring structure
[
140
,
148
]
. On the other hand, for an A-twisted theory, the equivariant quantum cohomology of the target space is the cohomology of the ordinary A-model supercharge, which by definition is the twisted chiral ring of the gauge theory
[
65
,
140
,
148
]
. Therefore, the full Bethe–Gauge correspondence would have to invoke the cohomology of A-model supercharge, as has been argued in
[
40
,
102
,
103
]
. Since the stable envelopes provide a map between quantum equivariant cohomology
43
43

43

Stable envelope map preserves the ring structure of the quantum equivariant cohomology in some weak sense
[
88
, Theorem 7.2.1]
.
of the fixed-point loci
44
44

44

This is the fixed-point loci of the flavor-symmetry action on the corresponding Higgs branch.
and the target, one would like to construct a basis for the twisted chiral ring which coincides with the stable basis for the equivariant quantum cohomology. Therefore, cohomological stable envelopes (
7.5
) are the corresponding A-model observables. As it is known, these generators are functions of the complex scalar in the vector multiplet of the 2d theory
[
28
]
.

Remark C.5

(identifying the A-model observables)
.

The A-model observables
\mathcal{O}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)
are functions of complex scalars
\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}
in the vector multiplet associated with the gauge invariance
[
28
]
. In the computation of the
R
-matrix, we use particular A-model observables obtained from the stable envelopes using the identification (
C.1
). Considering the identification (
C.1
) and the shifts (
C.3
), and using (
7.7
) and (
7.8
), the cohomological stable envelopes take the following form:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(p)={}

\displaystyle\text{Sym}_{S_{N}}\left[\prod_{a=1}^{N}\Biggl(\prod_{i<p(a)}\left(+\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\right)\prod_{i>p(a)}\left(-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}+m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\right)\Biggr)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\Biggl(\prod_{a>b}\frac{1}{\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{b}}\Biggr)\right].

(C.4)

This is the form of the cohomological stable envelopes for
\mathfrak{sl}(1|1)
that we use in the following. Note that these observables can be inserted anywhere on
S^{2}
and the final result is independent of the location of insertions
[
28
, Section 3.3]
.

Remark C.6

(Coulomb vs. Higgs branch localization schemes)
.

For GLSMs, there are two different localization schemes depending on the choice of the localizing action: the Higgs-branch localization and the Coulomb-branch localization
[
16
,
28
]
. These localization schemes are expected to give rise to the same final results but the explicit details and the localization procedure could be very different. The A-localization computation of this section is an example of the Coulomb-branch localization.

Remark C.7

(
R
-matrix from the A-model localization)
.

From the mathematical definition, it is expected that the
R
-matrix is related to the two-point function of stable envelopes
[
23
]
. As we briefly recall in Section
C.2
, the A-model localization computations involve a summation over all instanton sectors. However,
R
-matrix is insensitive to these non-perturbative corrections. As such, the computation of
R
-matrix reduces to the computation of a two-point function of stable envelopes in the zero-flux sector. We will further elaborate on the construction below.

Remark C.8

(interpretation in terms of
\mathcal{N}=4
quantum mechanics)
.

Since the computation of
R
-matrix is the restriction of certain two-point functions to zero-flux section, we can interpret the result in the corresponding quantum mechanics. This theory is obtained by the dimensional reduction of our 2d theory on
I\times S^{1}_{\text{C}}
. The insertion of A-model observables on
S^{2}
leads to certain boundary conditions in quantum mechanics, as illustrated in Figure
9
. Therefore, the two-point function of A-model observables in the zero-flux sector is the interval partition function of the corresponding
\mathcal{N}=4
quantum mechanics in the presence of these boundary conditions.

Having summarized all the necessary ingredients, we can now compute the
R
-matrix. In this construction, we use the cohomological stable envelopes (

).

Through (

Colliding these interfaces gives rise to a single interface:
\mathcal{J}(m_{\mathfrak{C}},0)\mathcal{J}(0,m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}})=\mathcal{J}(m_{\mathfrak{C}},m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}})
. Setting

The next ingredient is the formula for the computation of the two-point functions of GLSM observables on sphere
S^{2}
in the zero-flux sector. In the case of theory
\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\text{2d}}
, it is given by
[
28
]
(see Appendix
C.2.2
for the derivation)

\langle\mathcal{O}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\rangle_{S^{2},0}=\frac{1}{N!}\mathop{\text{\Large$\oint_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\text{JK}$}}$}}\nolimits\mathsf{d}\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Z}[\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}]\bigl(\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr),

(C.5)

where the subscript
0
denotes the zero-flux sector,
\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}=\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}_{N}\bigr)
,

\mathsf{d}\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathsf{d}\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\mathsf{d}\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{N},\qquad\mathcal{Z}[\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}]\bigl(\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\mathcal{Z}\bigl(\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}\bigl(\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\bigl(\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr),

and

\mathcal{Z}\bigl(\bm{\sigma}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\equiv\prod_{a=1}^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigl(-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}+m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\neq b\end{subarray}}^{N}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{b}\bigr).

(C.6)

The contribution to the integral is determined by the JK-pole prescription and comes from the contribution of chiral multiplets. The location of the poles is given by (
C.26
).

The A-model observables relevant for the computation of

]

46

46

In

\begin{gathered}\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;N,L}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi^{\prime};N,L}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0},\end{gathered}

(C.7)

where

\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;N,L}\propto\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(p),

(C.8)

Remark C.9

(normalization of observables)
.

Let us make a curious observation before proceeding to details. In the following computations, we demand that the observables
\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;N,L}
are orthonormal, meaning
\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;N,L}\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;N,L}\rangle=1
and
\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;N,L}\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi;N,L}\rangle=0
. If we do not stress on orthonormality of these observables, then we end up with a
4\times 4
matrix that all the components of the middle
2\times 2
diagonal block
47
47

47

For an
R
-matrix, this
2\times 2
block is the one-magnon sector
R
-matrix.
are multiplied by
\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{-1}
and the bottom
1\times 1
diagonal block
48
48

48

For an
R
-matrix, this
1\times 1
block is the two-magnon sector
R
-matrix.
is multiplied by
\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{-2}
. Such a matrix
does not
satisfy the Yang–Baxter equation and as such is not an
R
-matrix at all. In the case of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)
spin chains considered in
[
23
, Section 6.1]
, the analogous observables are already normalized and there is no need for a rescaling. The reason is a certain contribution of the adjoint chiral multiplet that takes care of the normalization. In the present case, there is no adjoint chiral multiplet and as such we need this rescaling.

\bm{R}
-matrix in the one-magnon sector. The one-magnon sector is described by setting
{N=1}
, hence there are two possible choices of the injective map, which we call
p
and
p^{\prime}
:
p(1)=1
or
p^{\prime}(1)=2
.
There are also two possible choices of permutation, which we denote by
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
:
\pi(\{1,2\})=\{1,2\}
and
\pi^{\prime}(\{1,2\})=\{2,1\}
. There are two complex twisted masses:
m^{\mathbb{C}}=\bigl(m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}},m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)
with
m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}=0
, and there is only one effective complex twisted mass
{m^{\mathbb{C}}\equiv m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}=-m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}}
. Similarly, there are two real parameters, defined in (
C.2
), with
{m_{1}+m_{2}=0}
, and a single effective parameter
m\equiv m_{1}=-m_{2}
. Correspondingly, there are two chambers
\mathfrak{C}=\{m>0\}
and
-\mathfrak{C}=\{m<0\}
. These chambers correspond to the permutations
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
. We then have

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}+m^{\mathbb{C}}_{2}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(+\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\right)=\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr),

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(+\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}+m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\right)=\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr).

The prefactor
\bigl(\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}
is chosen to guarantee the orthonormality of the observables
\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;N,L}
, which can be seen using (
C.5
) as follows

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=1,\qquad

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=0,

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=1,\qquad

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=0.

All these observables only depend on a single permutation
\pi
, which means that real masses are chosen in a single chamber, say
\mathfrak{C}
. Physically this means that there is no Janus interface, and these are just orthonormality conditions on the observables.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}.

These correlation functions involve two observables and in the presence of a Janus interface
\mathscr{J}(m_{\mathfrak{C}},m_{-\mathfrak{C}})
, the observables in the two sides of the interval depend on two different permutations
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
.
Hence, we find the one-magnon sector
R
-matrix constructed from the quantum mechanics

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt},(1)}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(C.9)

Remark C.10
.

By comparing this with
[
23
, equation (6.7)]
, we see that the one-magnon sector

\mathfrak{sl}(2)
and
\mathfrak{sl}(1|1)
. This is expected since the statistics of the excitation do not play any role in the one-magnon. We have seen this above but in a different disguise: the stable envelopes have been the same as we have explained in Remark
6.2
.

Finally,

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p^{\prime},\pi;1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p^{\prime},\pi^{\prime};1,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

that regarding (
C.9
) is just the inverse
\bigl(\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt},(1)}\bigr)^{-1}
of
\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt},(1)}
, as it is expected

\bigl(\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt},(1)}\bigr)^{-1}\bigl(m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}},m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Let us now compute the correlation functions (
C.7
) for the gauge theory corresponding to the two-magnon sector.

\bm{R}
-matrix in the two-magnon sector. The two-magnon sector is described by setting
{N=2}
. There is only one possible choice of the injective map
p(\{1,2\})=\{1,2\}
. As the Abelian case, there are two possible choices of permutation, which we denote by
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
:
\pi(\{1,2\})=\{1,2\}
and
\pi^{\prime}(\{1,2\})=\{2,1\}
. The complex masses and the real parameters are as in the Abelian case. We have
\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}=\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{1},\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{2}\bigr)
)

\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{\bigl(-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}+m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\bigr)\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{2}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\bigr)}{\sigma_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-\sigma_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}\right)+\bigl(\sigma_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\longleftrightarrow\sigma_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr),

and
\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi^{\prime};2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)
. Again, the prefactor
\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}
is determined by demanding orthogonality. Using (
C.5
), we see that

\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=1,

which means that
\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)
is normalized. Furthermore,

\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi^{\prime};2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}.

This gives the geometric
R
-matrix in the two-magnon sector

\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt},(2)}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}.

(C.10)

Finally,

\bigl\langle\mathcal{O}_{p,\pi;2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\mathcal{O}^{\star}_{p,\pi^{\prime};2,2}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{S^{2},0}=-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}},

which is just the inverse of (
C.10
).

Putting (
C.9
) and (
C.10
) together, the
R
-matrix of
\mathfrak{sl}(1|1)
superspin chain constructed through the Bethe–Gauge correspondence is

\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|cc|c}1&0&0&0\\
\hline\cr 0&-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&0\\[6.0pt]
0&\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}&0\\[6.0pt]
\hline\cr 0&0&0&-\frac{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}-\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{m_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}\end{array}\right).

If we identify
u\rightarrow m^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{2}
and
\hbar\rightarrow\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}
, we see from (
7.10
) that

\mathrm{R}^{\text{gt}}(u)=\mathrm{R}^{\text{RR}}(u)
.
Therefore, our A-model localization successfully reproduces the geometric
R
-matrix of
\mathfrak{sl}(1|1)
superspin chain, as it is expected from the Bethe/Gauge correspondence.

C.2

Further details on two-point functions of 2d GLSM observables

Here, we will collect concepts and formulas for the computation of two-point function of
\Omega
-deformed A-model observables. We follow
[
28
]
to which we refer for all the details.

C.2.1

Brief recap of Closset–Cremonesi–Park localization formula

Consider a 2d
\mathcal{N}(2,2)
GLSM with
\text{rank}(\mathfrak{g})=r
gauge Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, whose Cartan subalgebra we denote by
\mathfrak{h}
, in the
\Omega
-deformed sphere background
S^{2}_{\Omega}
.
49
49

49

We do not need the details of this background, which can be found in
[
28
, Section 2.1]
.
We denote the corresponding groups by
G
and
H
and the complexified Lie algebras by
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbb{C}}
, respectively. Different topological sectors are labeled by magnetic flux
\bm{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{r})\in\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{r}\subset\mathfrak{ih}
, where

\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\equiv\{\bm{n}\mid\rho(\bm{n}),\,\forall\rho\in\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}}\},

and
\rho(\bm{n})
is given by the canonical pairing of dual vector spaces and electric charges take value in
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}}
, the weight lattice. Supersymmetric observables belong to the cohomology of left- and right-moving supercharges
\mathsf{Q}
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}
. In the presence of
\Omega
-deformation, we have
\delta_{\mathsf{Q}}\sigma=\delta_{\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}}\sigma=0
when the vector field
V
, that generates the rotation isometry, vanishes. Hence such operators are functions of
\sigma
, the complex scalar field in the vector multiplet of the theory. Furthermore, they can only be inserted at the north and south poles of
S^{2}_{\Omega}
. When we turn off the
\Omega
background, they can be inserted anywhere on the sphere. The two-point function of such operators, which we denote by
\mathcal{O}^{N}(\sigma_{N})
and
\mathcal{O}^{S}(\sigma_{S})
for
N
and
S
denoting the north and south poles, are given by
[
28
]

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{O}^{N}(\sigma_{N})\mathcal{O}^{R}(\sigma_{R})\right\rangle_{S^{2}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(-1)^{\#}}{|W|}\sum_{\bm{n}\in\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}}\mathsf{q}^{\bm{n}}\mathop{\text{\Large$\oint_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\text{JK}$}}$}}\nolimits\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{$1$-loop}}(\sigma,\epsilon)\mathcal{O}^{N}\left(\sigma-\frac{\epsilon\bm{n}}{2}\right)\mathcal{O}^{S}\left(\sigma+\frac{\epsilon\bm{n}}{2}\right),

(C.11)

where
|W|
denotes the order of the Weyl group
W
of
\mathfrak{g}
,
\#
denotes the number of chiral multiplets with
R
-charge
2
,
50
50

50

The prefactor
(-1)^{\#}
is a sign ambiguity which is irrelevant for the rest of our discussion since we do not have a chiral multiplet of
R
-charge
+2
. The prescription to fix this sign ambiguity, which we have stated here, is given in
[
28
, Section 4.5]
.

\sigma
is the coordinate on
\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}\equiv\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{r}
, which is the cover of
\mathscr{M}\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}/W
, the Coulomb-branch moduli space, and
\epsilon
is the
\Omega
-deformation parameter.
\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{$1$-loop}}(\sigma)
is the 1-loop contribution to the partition function from the
\bm{n}
th
topological sector, which is give by

\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{$1$-loop}}(\sigma)=\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{vector}}(\sigma;\epsilon)\prod_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{chiral}_{i}}(\sigma;\epsilon),

where
\text{chiral}_{i}
denotes the
i
th
chiral multiplet. These contributions could be written explicitly using

Z_{p}(x,\epsilon)\equiv\begin{cases}\displaystyle\prod_{q=-\frac{p}{2}+1}^{\frac{p}{2}-1}(x+\epsilon q),&\quad p>1,\\
1,&\quad p=1,\\
\displaystyle\prod_{q=-\frac{|p|}{2}}^{\frac{|p|}{2}}(x+\epsilon q)^{-1},&\quad p<1.\end{cases}

(C.12)

For our purpose, we only need to know
Z_{0}(x,\epsilon=0)
and
Z_{2}(x,\epsilon=0)
for
\bm{n}=\bm{0}
. From (
C.12
), we have

Z_{2}(x,\epsilon=0)=x,\qquad Z_{0}(x,\epsilon=0)=\frac{1}{x}.

(C.13)

We then have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{vector}}(\sigma,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta}Z_{2-\alpha(\bm{n})}(\alpha(\sigma),\epsilon),

(C.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{chiral}_{i}}(\sigma,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{i}}Z_{q^{R}_{i}-\rho_{i}(\bm{n})}(\rho_{i}(\sigma)+m_{i},\epsilon).

In this formula,
\Delta
denotes the set of roots,
\rho_{i}
labels the weights of representation
\mathcal{R}_{i}
of
G
in which the
i
th
chiral multiplet transforms, and
m_{i}
and
q^{R}_{i}
denote the twisted mass and
U(1)_{V}

R
-symmetry charge of the
i
th
chiral multiplet. Defining the notation

\bigl(\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}},m_{\mathcal{I}},q^{R}_{\mathcal{I}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}\bigl(\rho_{i},m_{i},q^{R}_{i}\bigr),&\mathcal{I}=(i,\rho_{i}),\\
(\alpha,0,2),&\mathcal{I}=\alpha.\end{cases}

(C.15)

We can write the 1-loop partition function as follows:
51
51

51

Considering Figure
9
, we only need the zero-flux sector of the GLSM observables in the computation of interval partition function of quantum mechanics, as we will see momentarily.

\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{1-loop}}(\sigma,\epsilon)=\prod_{\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{n})-q^{R}_{\mathcal{I}}}(\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}(\sigma)+m_{\mathcal{I}},\epsilon).

(C.16)

The parameter
\mathsf{q}
in (
C.11
) is related to the complexified FI parameters, defined as follows:

\eta^{I}\equiv\frac{\theta^{I}}{2\pi}+\mathfrak{i}\zeta^{I}\in\mathbb{C},\qquad 1\leq I\leq\dim(\mathfrak{c}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}),

where
\theta^{I}
and
\zeta^{I}
are the theta angle and real FI parameters. They belong to the dual of the complexified center
\mathfrak{c}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}
and could be thought of as elements of
\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}
through the embedding
{\mathfrak{c}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}}
. We denote the image of the physical FI parameters
\zeta^{I}
under this embedding by
{\zeta^{a},a=1,\dots,r}
. Then,

\mathsf{q}^{\bm{n}}\equiv\exp\bigl(2\pi\mathfrak{i}\sum_{I}\bm{\eta}\cdot\bm{n}\bigr)
,
where
n_{I}
denotes the flux in the free
I
th

\U(1)
part of the center of
G
. Note that
\bm{n}\in\mathfrak{i\mathfrak{h}}
and hence
\bm{\eta}\cdot\bm{n}
is given by the canonical pairing between
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Finally, we need to specify the contour of integration, which is given by the JK contour
[
21
,
68
,
137
]
. The poles of
\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{1-loop}}(\sigma,\epsilon)
are the loci of intersections of
s\geq r
hyperplanes
\bigl\{\mathcal{H}^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{H}^{k_{s}}_{\mathcal{I}_{s}}\bigr\}
, where

\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\mathcal{I}}\equiv\left\{\sigma\,\biggl|\,\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}(\sigma)=-m_{\mathcal{I}}-\left(k+\frac{q^{R}_{\mathcal{I}}-\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{n})}{2}\right)\epsilon\right\},\qquad k\in\bigl[0,\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{n})-q^{R}_{\mathcal{I}}\bigr]_{\text{int}},

(C.17)

and
[a,b]_{\text{int}}
denotes the set of integers between
a
and
b
. There are two types of singularities: the non-degenerate singularities corresponding to the intersection of exactly
r
hyperplanes and the degenerate ones corresponding to the intersection of
s>r
hyperplanes. This complex-codimension-
r
singular loci is denoted as
\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{\text{sing}}_{\bm{n}}\subset\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}
. Note that
\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\mathcal{I}}
is a singular locus of
\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{1-loop}}
only when
\mathcal{I}
labels a chiral multiplet field.
\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\alpha}
labels the codimension-1 poles with zero residue. The definition of JK residues at
\sigma_{*}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\bm{n}}^{\text{sing}}
depends on an additional parameter
\eta\in\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, which is defined as follows. The charge
\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}
, defined in (
C.15
), belongs to
\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
and hence any set of charges
\{\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\dots,\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}_{r}}\}
generates a cone

\text{Con}[\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\dots,\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}_{r}}]\subset\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

(C.18)

The set of all such cones can be divided into dimension-
r
chambers that are separated by codimension-
1
walls. The chamber to FI parameters
\xi^{a}\in\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*},a=1,\dots,r
belong to determines the phase of the GLSM
[
91
,
147
]
. The effective FI parameter
\zeta^{\text{UV},I}_{\text{eff}}
at the infinity (i.e., when
{|\sigma|\rightarrow\infty}
) of Coulomb branch
\mathscr{M}
is defined to be

\zeta^{\text{UV},a}_{\text{eff}}\equiv\zeta^{a}+\frac{b^{a}}{2\pi}\lim_{\mathsf{R}\rightarrow\infty}\ln\mathsf{R},\qquad b^{a}\equiv\sum_{i}\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{R}_{i}}(t^{a})=\sum_{\mathcal{I}}\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}.

t^{a}
denotes the generator of the
a
th

\mathfrak{u}(1)\subset\mathfrak{g}
,
52
52

52

t^{a}
is the image of the
I
th

\mathfrak{u}(1)
generator of the center of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
under the embedding
\mathfrak{c}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.
and the last equality follows from embedding
{\mathfrak{c}^{*}\subset\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}}
. Hence, phases of the theory on the Coulomb branch depend on the chamber
{\mathfrak{C}\subset\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}}
that the parameter
\zeta^{\text{UV}}_{\text{eff}}
belong to. This can be stated as follows:
\zeta^{\text{UV}}_{\text{eff}}
belongs to a chamber
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
if

\zeta+\frac{b}{2\pi}\ln\mathsf{R}\in\mathcal{C}
,
\forall\mathsf{R}\geq\mathsf{R}_{*}
,
for some
\mathsf{R}_{*}
, which always exists for practical purposes
[
28
]
. When
b\neq 0
, there could be two cases: 1) either
b
belongs to a definite chamber
\mathcal{C}
; In this case,
\zeta
is irrelevant and the theory has only one phase determined by the chamber
\mathcal{C}
, or 2)
b
belongs to a wall where chambers
{\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{p}}
meet; In this case, one can turn on
\zeta
and depending on its value,
\zeta^{\text{UV}}_{\text{eff}}
could belong to any of the chambers
\mathfrak{C}_{i},i=1,\dots,p
, which in turn determines the phase of the theory. When
b=0
, and hence the IR theory is a CFT, then
{\zeta^{\text{UV}}_{\text{eff}}=\zeta}
could be taken to be in any chamber
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathfrak{i}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. We then set

\xi\equiv\zeta^{\text{FI}}_{\text{eff}}.

(C.19)

The JK residues can now be defined as follows. If a singular point
\sigma_{*}
comes from the intersection of
s\geq r
hyperplanes
\bigl\{\mathcal{H}^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{H}^{k_{s}}_{\mathcal{I}_{s}}\bigr\}
, we denote the set of
\mathcal{H}
-charges as
\bm{\mathscr{W}}(\sigma_{*})\equiv\{\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\dots,\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}_{s}}\}
. The JK residues at
\sigma_{*}=\{\sigma_{*,1},\dots,\sigma_{*,r}\}
is then give by

\displaystyle\left.\text{JK-Res}[\bm{\mathscr{W}}(\sigma),\xi]\prod_{\mathscr{W}_{j}\in\bm{\mathscr{W}}_{r}}\frac{\mathsf{d}\sigma_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathsf{d}\sigma_{r}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}(\sigma)\cdots\mathscr{W}_{r}(\sigma)}\right|_{\sigma=\sigma_{*}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{cases}\dfrac{1}{|{\det}(\mathscr{W}_{i})|},&\xi\in\text{Con}[\mathscr{W}_{1},\dots,\mathscr{W}_{r}],\\
0,&\xi\notin\text{Con}[\mathscr{W}_{1},\dots,\mathscr{W}_{r}],\end{cases}

(C.20)

where
\bm{\mathscr{W}}_{r}
is any set among
s\choose r
sets of
r

\mathfrak{h}
-charges in
\bm{\mathscr{W}}(\sigma_{*})
. This concludes our discussion of the necessary results from
[
28
]
.

C.2.2

Explicit computation of two-point functions

We would like to compute the contribution of vector and chiral multiplets to the correlation functions (
C.7
), given in (
C.11
) or more compactly in (
C.16
), explicit.
For the fields in the vector multiplet,
\mathcal{I}=(i,\rho_{i})\equiv(ab,\rho_{ab})
, as (
C.15
)

\rho_{ab}=\alpha_{ab}=e_{a}-e_{b}
,
a\neq b
,
where
\alpha_{ab}
denotes the
ab
th
root of
\mathfrak{u}(N)
, associated to the generator
E_{ab}
, and
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{N}\}
is the standard basis for
\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
53
53

53

For
\alpha_{ab}=e_{a}-e_{b}
for
a\neq b
, we consider the one-dimensional vector space generated by
E_{ab}
. This space is an eigenspace of the Cartan subalgebra with eigenvalue
\alpha_{ab}
. These eigenspaces together with the Cartan subalgebra generate
\mathfrak{u}(N)
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\rho_{ab}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)=\sigma_{a}^{\mathbb{C}}-\sigma_{b}^{\mathbb{C}},\qquad a\neq b,

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{n})=\rho_{ab}(\bm{n})=n_{a}-n_{b},\qquad a\neq b.

(C.21)

Similarly, for chiral fields in the fundamental
(+)
and antifundamental
(-)
representations, the weights are (
\mathcal{I}=(i,\rho_{i})\equiv(a,\rho_{a,\pm})
as in (
C.15
))

\rho_{a,\pm}=\pm e_{a},\qquad a=1,\dots,N,

(C.22)

and hence for the chiral multiplets
54
54

54

We use the same notation for chiral multiplets of the theory
\mathscr{T}^{\prime\prime}_{\text{2d}}
in Figure
8
as the ones for
\mathscr{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\text{3d}}
and its dimensional reductions to 2d and 1d.

\bigl(\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}},\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bigr)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\colon\ \mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)+m_{\mathcal{I}}=\rho_{a,+}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)+m_{\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.87215pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.87215pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.32147pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.66785pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.66785pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.86128pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\sigma_{a}^{\mathbb{C}}-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\colon\ \mathscr{W}_{\mathcal{I}}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)+m_{\mathcal{I}}=\rho_{a,-}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)+m_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.66785pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.66785pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.86128pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.87215pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.87215pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.32147pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}+m_{i}^{\mathbb{C}}+\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}.

Using (
C.13
) and (
C.14
),
the contribution of vector multiplet is
55
55

55

Note that the contribution of vector multiplet is the same as an adjoint chiral with
R
-charge
+2

[
28
]
. Hence, we have taken
q_{\text{vector}}^{R}=2
.

\mathcal{Z}^{\text{vector}}_{2}(\sigma,\epsilon=0)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b=1\\
a\neq b\end{subarray}}^{N}\bigl(\sigma_{a}^{\mathbb{C}}-\sigma_{b}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr).

(C.23)

Similarly, setting
q_{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}=q_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{R}=0
, we obtain the contribution of chiral multiplets

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\mathcal{Q}}(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}};\epsilon=0)=\prod_{a=1}^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\bigl(\sigma_{a}^{\mathbb{C}}-m_{i}^{\mathbb{C}}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\bigr)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}}(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}};\epsilon=0)=\prod_{a=1}^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\bigl(-\sigma_{a}^{\mathbb{C}}+m_{i}^{\mathbb{C}}+\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}\bigr)}.

(C.24)

Putting together (
C.23
) and (
C.24
) and using (
C.11
), we thus see that the zero-flux sector contribution to the sphere partition function can be written explicitly as
56
56

56

As we mentioned above, there is no overall
(-1)^{\#}
sign since there is no chiral multiplet of
R
-charge
+2
in our construction.
(
{\bm{n}=0}
in (
C.11
))

\mathcal{Z}\equiv\langle 1\rangle_{S^{2},0}=\frac{1}{N!}\mathop{\text{\Large$\oint_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\text{JK}$}}$}}\nolimits\mathsf{d}\sigma_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\wedge\dots\wedge\mathsf{d}\sigma_{N}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Z}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{1},\dots,\sigma_{N}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr),

where

\mathcal{Z}\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{1},\dots,\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{N}\bigr)\equiv\prod_{a=1}^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\bigl(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\bigl(-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}+m^{\mathbb{C}}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\neq b\end{subarray}}^{N}(\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}_{b}).

(C.25)

The factor
1/N!
comes from the order of the Weyl-group factor in (
C.11
). This proves (
C.6
). Substituting (
C.25
) in (
C.11
) gives (
C.5
). The poles of JK prescription for the positive (effective) FI parameter (
\xi>0
, where
\xi
is defined in (
C.19
)) are given by

\displaystyle\text{JK Poles}\colon\ \sigma_{a}^{\mathbb{C}}=m_{i}^{\mathbb{C}}-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2},\qquad a=1,\dots,N,\qquad i=1,\dots,L,

(C.26)

coming from the intersection of hyperplanes
\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
C.17
) associated with fundamental chiral multiplets
\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
. Note that we need to take the contribution from all poles, i.e., all pairs
(a,i)
. In the following, we provide further details on this choice of JK poles in the computation of
R
-matrix.

Jeffrey–Kirwan poles for the computation of
\bm{R}
-matrix.
Let us give the details of the choice (
C.26
) of JK poles necessary for the computation of
R
-matrix in
C.1
. Although we have stated the poles in (
C.26
), we give more details on this choice using the formalism of
[
28
]
, as reviewed in Section
C.2
. As we have explained there, the choice of JK poles depends on the choice of the (effective) FI parameter
\xi
, defined in (
C.19
).

For the
1
-magnon sector, we have a
\U(1)
gauge theory with
\SU(2)
fundamental
\mathcal{Q}^{i}
and antifundamental
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
flavors. Using (
C.22
) and (
C.17
), the equations of hyperplanes (
C.17
) are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{k^{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Q}^{1}}}=\left\{\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\,\biggl|\,\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}=m^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}-\left(k_{1}^{\mathcal{Q}}-\frac{n}{2}\right)\epsilon\right\},\qquad k_{1}^{\mathcal{Q}}\in[0,n]_{\text{int}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{k^{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Q}^{2}}}=\left\{\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\,\biggl|\,\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}=m^{\mathbb{C}}_{2}-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}-\left(k_{2}^{\mathcal{Q}}-\frac{n}{2}\right)\epsilon\right\},\qquad k_{2}^{\mathcal{Q}}\in[0,n]_{\text{int}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{k^{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}}=\left\{\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}\,\biggl|\,-\sigma^{\mathbb{C}}=-m^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}-\frac{\hbar^{\mathbb{C}}}{2}-\left(k_{1}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}}+\frac{n}{2}\right)\epsilon\right\},\qquad k_{1}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}}\in[0,n]_{\text{int}},
